./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md-2025-@96 failed with args = 82,386,616,320 B; @128 showed 109,565,706,240.
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md-2026-Ratio 1.3299 vs nd ratio 128/96 = 1.3333 (0.3 %). So each process's
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md-2027-compiled program carries per-device-STACKED arguments (~858 MB per
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md-2028-device entry at LL2304 L20), and the earlier "@64 acceptance PASS" is
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md:2029:REINTERPRETED: 64 x ~858 MB ~= 54.9 GB simply sits UNDER the 63.8 GB
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md-2030-XLA arg limit — the #1370 fix reduced device RESIDENCY but did not
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md-2031-remove the per-device-stacked args from the program signature.
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md-2032-PLAUSIBLE candidates for the stacked list (uninspected): the per-band
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md-2033-vertex-mask cache or a per-device geometry stack. Next instrument: the
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md:2034:existing arg/buffer dump probe (scripts/tmp/_bufdump_compileonly.py,
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md:2035:job pattern oc128_bufdump.sbatch) at the @96 config to NAME the
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md-2036-arguments — then a targeted fix (shard-local slices as args, or
./docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md-2037-donate/rematerialise) unblocks oc at 96-224 GPUs.

--- campaign document excerpts ---
1253-
1254-* **Genuine device-count cost:** at the BEST partition, 4x the ranks at
1255-  identical per-rank work still costs **1.65x**. Not partition quality,
1256-  not load imbalance — a real rank-count term the atmosphere lane does
1257-  not have.
1258-* **Partition quality degrades WITH rank count:** sfc costs 1.22x at 32
1259-  ranks but **1.97x** at 128. So the two effects compound, and the
1260-  default `auto` is doing well to land near geometric.
1261-* Practical: pin `--partition-method geometric` (or auto) on this lane;
1262-  sfc is actively harmful at scale. pymetis is absent from `.venv-mpi`,
1263-  so the low-cut METIS arm codex wanted is still unmeasured.
1264-
1265:**512-RANK LADDER (job 26508063):** s7 63.83 ms, s8 194.80 ms.
1266-s8 keeps gaining 256->512 (254.41 -> 194.80 = **1.31x**, at 1 280
1267:cells/rank) while s7 goes flat (65.71 -> 63.83 = 1.03x, at 320
1268-cells/rank — below the 300-600 floor). Same floor as the atmosphere,
1269-reached at a different rank count because the mesh differs. So this lane
1270-DOES scale to 512 ranks when the mesh is large enough; it just pays the
1271-rank-count term on the way.
1272-
1273-UNEXPLAINED, flagged not resolved: s7's per-doubling speedup RISES
1274-(1.29 / 1.43 / 1.57) even in the controlled ladder. Efficiency improving
1275-as the tile shrinks has no physical mechanism I can name; the likeliest
1276-reading is that the np32 base point is anomalously slow (partition
1277-quality at low rank counts?) rather than the high-rank points being
1278-good. Under codex review (round 15) along with the discriminating
1279-experiment for device-count cost vs partition degradation.
--
2011-bytes censused, partition quality / placement / NCCL protocol
2012-receipted null.
2013-Remaining candidates are structural (per-CP effective launch/sync
2014-overhead chains, jitter amplification across dependency-chained
2015-collectives) — the arXiv:2607.16100 device-side collective API class,
2016-NOT reachable from today's XLA/NCCL stack. Together with the ensemble
2017-receipt (co-execution penalty 0.6 %), the campaign's operational
2018-conclusion: single-trajectory strong scaling at small-to-mid tiles is
2019-at the practical limit of the current stack; throughput past the floor
2020-comes from replicas, resolution, or upstream/runtime work.

2027-compiled program carries per-device-STACKED arguments (~858 MB per
2028-device entry at LL2304 L20), and the earlier "@64 acceptance PASS" is
2029:REINTERPRETED: 64 x ~858 MB ~= 54.9 GB simply sits UNDER the 63.8 GB
2030-XLA arg limit — the #1370 fix reduced device RESIDENCY but did not
2031:remove the per-device-stacked args from the program signature.
2032-PLAUSIBLE candidates for the stacked list (uninspected): the per-band

a=82386616320
b=109565706240
print('ratio', b/a)
print('relative delta %',(128/96/(b/a)-1)*100)

print('b per device',b/128,'decimal MB',b/128/1e6,'MiB',b/128/2**20)
print('64 extrapolation bytes',a/96*64,'GB',a/96*64/1e9,'GiB',a/96*64/2**30)

.physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md-738-
.physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md:739:2. [Ensemble inference is too broad](docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md:1973): the arithmetic is right: mean solo = 13.89 ms; 13.97 / 13.89 = 1.00576, or 0.576% maximum observed slowdown—17.4× below the 10% bar. .physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md-740-
.physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md-741-3. [The controls are placement-matched only for rep1](docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md:1967): solo_pre/post share rep1’s node set. rep2 and rep3 lack same-node solo brackets, so their comparison can include placement differences. Say “no detected total co-execution penalty within this three-replica experiment,” not “no measurable fabric contention.”
.physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md-742-
.physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md:743:4. [The sick-node conclusion overreaches and omits the completed probe](docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md:1982): add 26641282: failure reproduced on rep0’s eight nodes while rep2’s other eight passed. This identifies a failing eight-node allocation versus a passing comparator; it does not identify a single sick node or prove the same cause as the LL hangs. A shutdown-barrier deadline also is not, by itself, the same post-init deadlock class.
.physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md-744-
.physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md-745-5. [Transport proof is correctly reported but over-scoped](docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md:1915): 258 NET/IB lines, zero Socket lines, and LL512@8 = 3.5995 ms support the two-node @8 probe (3.60 ms rounded). They do not prove GDRDMA behavior—or rule out transport/topology effects—on all 32-node / 128-GPU directive runs.
.physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md-746-
.physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md-747-6. .physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md-748-
.physics-validator/scaling_campaign/codex_r11_2026-08-03.md:749:7. * **Matched-tile gap decomposition (topology-correct census)**: the
  s8@16 -> s9@64 measured gap is +3.17 ms; the MODELED comm growth

  MPAS combining test would need its own arm (and may not be
  XLA-combinable at all).

### Transport discriminator (job 26638578): NCCL runs NET/IB + GDRDMA — the gap is real

The early_init "no NCCL net plugin visible -> likely TCP sockets"
warning fired on every multi-node lane; NCCL_DEBUG=INFO on the real
bench (atm latlon @8 over 2 nodes) shows **258 NET/IB channel lines,
including NET/IB/…/GDRDMA, and zero NET/Socket** — NCCL's BUILT-IN IB
verbs transport is active (no plugin needed; the warning is a
plugin-visibility heuristic written for Derecho and is a FALSE ALARM on
Levante at this scale — softening it is a follow-up). SCOPE (codex
r11): this proves the 2-node/8-GPU case; it makes socket fallback on
the 32-node/128-GPU runs UNLIKELY (same env, same stack) but does not
directly measure them, nor rule out topology-scale transport effects
there. The mechanism hunt (per-CP effective overhead / scheduling)
continues. Bonus receipt: LL512x1024 @8 over 2 nodes =
3.60 ms (probe row, steps 12).

### s10@128 blocked by the #1100 remainder (job 26630207)

FAILED with `byte size of input/output arguments (162,319,564,800)
exceeds the base limit` — the MPAS MESH is still global per process
(state is partition-local since #1100; the mesh's SFC-partition-local
construction is the documented open remainder, quoted in the bench
source). At subdiv-10 the global geometry stacks alone exceed XLA's
arg limit, so 128-224-GPU MPAS at s10 is BLOCKED on that feature —
engineering follow-up, not an env bug. The hundreds-of-GPUs MPAS
receipt therefore stands at subdiv-9/128 GPUs.

### CP-combining A/B: three deadlocks on one node block; pivoted to @64 with exclusion

Jobs 26630576 / 26638830 / 26640037 (LL2048@128 A/B) all hung in arm A
on the SAME 32-node block (l50000...l50187) and timed out with zero
receipts. Evidence collected in place: all 64 IB ports ACTIVE; NCCL
per-rank debug (v4) shows ALL 128 ranks reach "Connected all rings"
then every python idles at ~1 % CPU with memory preallocated — a TRUE
DEADLOCK after NCCL init (plausibly the pre-loop global barrier);
/dev/shm on l50000 clean (stale-segment hypothesis REFUTED); no stray
processes. The SAME code path ran LL2048@128 fine on 2026-07-30 (job
26534060, different nodes) and MPAS s9@128 on 2026-08-01 — so
block-correlation is PLAUSIBLE (cause unknown; candidate: switch-level
path issue invisible to port state). Excluding all 32 leaves only 24
a100_80 nodes, below a 32-node ask — so the lever test moved to
**LL2048@64 (16 nodes, block excluded), job 26641073**: the gap is
2.35x there, and a >=10 % combine win at @64 confirms the lever without
the sick block. If a later job hangs on the block again -> DKRZ ticket
with this section as the evidence.

## ENSEMBLE RECEIPT (job 26628196): lever #1 CONFIRMED — co-execution penalty <= 0.6 %

steps=5000 protocol (window ~70 s; absolute ms NOT comparable to the
steps-12 ladder rows by design). Same-job arms, disjoint 8-node sets,
per-step nodelists logged:

| arm | nodes | ms/step | GC/s |
|---|---|---|---|
| solo_pre | l50042-l50072 set | 13.86 | 4.92 |
| rep1 (concurrent) | l50042-l50072 set | 13.92 | 4.90 |
| rep2 (concurrent) | l50000-l50039 set | 13.86 | 4.92 |
| rep3 (concurrent) | l50115-l50187 set | 13.97 | 4.88 |
| solo_post | l50042-l50072 set | 13.92 | 4.89 |

* **max(replica)/mean(solo) = 13.97/13.89 = 1.006** (0.58 % max
  observed slowdown; the 1.10 bar is cleared 17x over): NO DETECTED
  co-execution penalty WITHIN THIS THREE-REPLICA EXPERIMENT. Aggregate
  ~2.994x solo for K=3. Caveats (codex r11): only rep1 shares the solo
  brackets' node set (rep2/rep3 comparisons include placement
  differences), and K=4+ linearity is untested (rep0's set failed).
* Consequence (lever #1, scoped): at K=3 the measured aggregate is
  ~14.7 GC/s from 96 GPUs vs 5.9 single-trajectory at 128 — replicas
  deliver ~3x more science throughput in this experiment. Extrapolation
  to K=4 (~19.6 GC/s) is PLAUSIBLE, not measured (the 4th set failed
  for unrelated node reasons).
* rep0 (4th replica) ABORTED at 7:05 on nodes l[50075,50078,50081,
  50100,50103,50106,50109,50112] with a Shutdown-barrier
  DEADLINE_EXCEEDED. Probe 26641282 REPRODUCED failure on rep0's
  exact 8 nodes while rep2's 8 passed — this identifies a FAILING
  8-node ALLOCATION vs a passing comparator; it does not by itself
  name a single node, nor prove the same cause as the LL2048@128
  hangs (a shutdown-barrier deadline is a different observable than
  the all-idle hang). Single-sick-node attribution = PLAUSIBLE
  hypothesis under bisection (halves queued); DKRZ ticket once
  narrowed.

### CP-combining A/B verdict (job 26641073, LL2048@64, sick block excluded): REFUTED

A 6.740 / B combine-8MB 6.682 (-0.86 %) / C combine+pipelined 6.689
(-0.76 %) / A2 6.648 (-1.37 %) ms. B and C sit inside the single
A<->A2 control bracket: **no >=10 % benefit detected at @64 for these
flags**. Per the pre-registered interpretation limit this is a
threshold/implementation null (one control bracket, no GPU post-pass
CP census per arm) — not a general refutation of CP combinability. Reproduction note:
arm A matches the 26502539 receipt (6.732) at 6.740 across
days/node-sets — the lane is highly repeatable.

**Standing bottleneck picture for the ~2.4-4.5x-above-model gaps at
healthy tiles**: transport fallback UNLIKELY (NET/IB+GDRDMA proven at
2-node scale), no detected win from CP-combining at this threshold,
bytes censused, partition quality / placement / NCCL protocol
receipted null.
Remaining candidates are structural (per-CP effective launch/sync
overhead chains, jitter amplification across dependency-chained
collectives) — the arXiv:2607.16100 device-side collective API class,
NOT reachable from today's XLA/NCCL stack. Together with the ensemble
receipt (co-execution penalty 0.6 %), the campaign's operational
conclusion: single-trajectory strong scaling at small-to-mid tiles is
at the practical limit of the current stack; throughput past the floor
comes from replicas, resolution, or upstream/runtime work.


### Ocean @96 bracket (job 26642771): the resident-args size is LINEAR in device count

@96 failed with args = 82,386,616,320 B; @128 showed 109,565,706,240.
Ratio 1.3299 vs nd ratio 128/96 = 1.3333 (0.3 %). So each process's
compiled program carries per-device-STACKED arguments (~858 MB per
device entry at LL2304 L20), and the earlier "@64 acceptance PASS" is
REINTERPRETED: 64 x ~858 MB ~= 54.9 GB simply sits UNDER the 63.8 GB
XLA arg limit — the #1370 fix reduced device RESIDENCY but did not
remove the per-device-stacked args from the program signature.
PLAUSIBLE candidates for the stacked list (uninspected): the per-band
vertex-mask cache or a per-device geometry stack. Next instrument: the
existing arg/buffer dump probe (scripts/tmp/_bufdump_compileonly.py,
job pattern oc128_bufdump.sbatch) at the @96 config to NAME the
arguments — then a targeted fix (shard-local slices as args, or
donate/rematerialise) unblocks oc at 96-224 GPUs.

per128=855982080 B = 855.982080 MB = 816.328125 MiB
64 extrap=54.924411 GB = 51.152344 GiB

"""Compile-only #1370-bis probe: dump buffer assignment, never execute."""
import os, sys
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "bench"))
import jax
import numpy as np

def main():
    n_lat, n_lon, nlev, nd = 2304, 4608, 20, 128
    from bench_ocean_latlon_spmd_scaling import build_model_and_state
    from legoesm.ocean.dynamics.sharded_ocean_step import (
        make_sharded_ocean_step, shard_state_latlon)
    from jax.sharding import Mesh

    with jax.default_device(jax.local_devices(backend="cpu")[0]):
        model, s0 = build_model_and_state(n_lat, n_lon, nlev,
                                          baro_solver="implicit_cn",
                                          force_pcg=True)
        model._ensure_vertex_mask(s0)
    mesh = Mesh(np.array(jax.devices()[:nd]), ("lat",))
    s = shard_state_latlon(s0, mesh)
    del s0
    step = make_sharded_ocean_step(model, mesh)
    print("lowering + compiling (no execution)...", flush=True)
    c = jax.jit(step).lower(s, 150.0).compile()
    try:
        ma = c.memory_analysis()
        print(f"args={ma.argument_size_in_bytes/1e9:.2f}GB "
              f"out={ma.output_size_in_bytes/1e9:.2f}GB "
              f"temp={ma.temp_size_in_bytes/1e9:.2f}GB", flush=True)
    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001
        print("memory_analysis:", e)
    return 0

if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
#!/bin/bash -l
#SBATCH --job-name=bufdump
#SBATCH --partition=compute
#SBATCH --nodes=1
#SBATCH --mem=0
#SBATCH --time=03:00:00
#SBATCH --output=bufdump2.%j.log
# Name the n_dev-proportional buffer that kills LL2304@128 (203 GiB in
# BOTH solver arms — wide-halo refuted as the cause). Compile the same
# partitioning on 128 VIRTUAL CPU devices and dump the HLO + buffer
# assignment; device count is a compile-time property, so the logical
# buffers match what the GPUs would allocate (same method that cracked
# the np4 fusion). Single node, no GPU queue.
set -uo pipefail
SUBMIT_DIR="${SLURM_SUBMIT_DIR:-$(pwd)}"
export JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu
source "${SUBMIT_DIR}/scripts/cluster/scaling_levante/_env.sh"
cd "$REPO"
OUTDIR="${OUTDIR:-$SCRATCH/legoesm_scaling/bufdump_j${SLURM_JOB_ID}}"
mkdir -p "$OUTDIR"; echo "outdir=$OUTDIR"
export XLA_FLAGS="--xla_force_host_platform_device_count=128 --xla_dump_to=$OUTDIR/hlo --xla_dump_hlo_as_text"
timeout 9000 "$PY" scripts/tmp/_bufdump_compileonly.py \
  || echo "(failure after the dump is still a success — the dump is the product)"
echo "=== biggest allocations in buffer assignment ==="
B=$(ls "$OUTDIR"/hlo/*buffer-assignment* 2>/dev/null | head -1)
[ -n "$B" ] && grep -oE "size [0-9]+" "$B" | sort -t' ' -k2 -rn | head -5
echo "=== parameter shapes of the biggest module ==="
F=$(ls -S "$OUTDIR"/hlo/*after_optimizations.txt 2>/dev/null | grep -v buffer | head -1)
[ -n "$F" ] && grep -m1 "entry_computation_layout" "$F" | grep -oE "f32\[[0-9,]+\]" | sort | uniq -c | sort -rn | head -8
echo DONE

codex
The arithmetic is sound. I also found two prior-scope regressions plus a labeling/config mismatch in the new section that need correction before it can ship.
codex
The new arithmetic is correct: 1.3298969 rounds to 1.3299; it is 0.258% below the ideal 1.3333333. The inferred @64 total is 54.924 GB, below 63.8 GB.

1. The central stacked-arguments claim is not labelled **PLAUSIBLE**. Only its candidate buffers are. Make the heading/body say the *observed compiler-reported total is near-linear*, and mark the per-device-stacked interpretation PLAUSIBLE. [section](< /work/bd1083/b309178/diffESM/legoesm_pg/legoESM/.claude/worktrees/scaling-campaign/docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md:2023>)

2. The named bufdump script is currently hard-coded to 128 virtual devices, as is its sbatch file; it is not an “@96 config” yet. Say it will be adapted to @96, or make that configuration change first. [probe](</work/bd1083/b309178/diffESM/legoesm_pg/legoESM/.claude/worktrees/scaling-campaign/scripts/tmp/_bufdump_compileonly.py:9>)

3. The prior s10 correction was not applied: it still states “128–224 GPU … BLOCKED,” rather than observed-blocked at 128 and 192/224 expected-blocked. [s10 section](</work/bd1083/b309178/diffESM/legoesm_pg/legoESM/.claude/worktrees/scaling-campaign/docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md:1938>)

4. The A/B body is correctly a threshold null with the stated percentages, but its heading still says “REFUTED,” conflicting with “not a general refutation.” [A/B heading](</work/bd1083/b309178/diffESM/legoesm_pg/legoESM/.claude/worktrees/scaling-campaign/docs/performance/scaling/levante_campaign_2026-07-24.md:1997>)
